Initially, we had proposed an amendment setting the tax threshold at 1.4 tonnes, but the principle of taxation has led to a bronca on the part of many elected representatives, including the rapporteur-general, who opposed it. Then, as a miracle, the Government seemed to go back, considering that it was ultimately not uninteresting, in terms of vehicle consumption, to integrate the concept of weight, while excluding family vehicles, in order to optimize taxation. Finally, the Government accepted the principle of taxation but not its effects, since with a threshold of 1.8 tons, as proposed in its amendment, only 2 per cent of vehicles in use are affected, and only one SUV. Amendment No 888 is intended to put the threshold at 1.3 tonnes, as proposed in the Citizens' Convention. Beyond the principle of taxation, this threshold would indeed benefit from the effects of taxation and put an end to the hypocrisy expressed in relation to the proposals of the Convention.

I would point out that the European Commission has granted this exemption to the maritime sector, by expressly limiting it to the European maritime sector: this measure is therefore authorised by Brussels. Let French sailors be subjected to unbridled international competition, why not – after all, we are used to it – but in this case it is the competition of European flags that we have to face. Italy, Denmark and Cyprus, for example, give their shipowners net weight. All over the world, similar arrangements have multiplied under the benevolent eye of the European authorities. We therefore want Article 15a B to be retained, so that the French flag is exempt from social, employers' and wage charges.

Unsurprisingly, it appears that the departments are experiencing the crisis at full speed, with a scissor effect: their revenues are decreasing, especially DMTO – transfer fees for a fee – while their expenditure is increasing, especially those related to the RSA – income from active solidarity. The communal bloc, however, is not spared because of the sharp decline in state and tariff revenues, in particular. The safety net we voted for in the PLFR is therefore not sufficient and only partially corrects these decreases. The Court of Auditors therefore anticipates a drop in gross savings of around 30 %, a much greater use of debt and, consequently, a deterioration in the capacity for deleveraging, in particular for the communal bloc: it would increase from an average of five years in 2019 to seven years in 2020. I am particularly concerned about the impact on the investment of local communities. The Court of Auditors notes that the investment of the communal bloc is already declining, hence my strong concerns for 2021. These four amendments have only one aim: to improve management savings. The state has relied heavily on local authorities, including departments, to deal with the crisis, in particular to ensure certain supplies, such as masks, and all the money that the state owes to the departments is not compensated – we are talking about 4 billion euros of unpaid payments linked to the RSA, or which, at least, have not been transferred to the nearest euro. We can therefore think that, given the policy that is being pursued and the lack of state relief in the area of poverty, this phenomenon will only increase in 2021, and I do not know why we want to question the deletion of the article voted by the Senate or why, in this situation, we will meggle out of 1 billion euros in favour of the departments.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u506	Mr Yannick Kerlogot has the floor to support Amendment No. 521.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u507	This last chance amendment, which deals with a regional issue, is defended by seventeen Members sitting on various benches in the Chamber and reflects a call from the national representation for solidarity with Breton farmers, who have been suffering considerable and steadily increasing damage for three years, caused by a protected yet invasive species: the slug of towers. The problem is known and, beyond the proliferation of public health and safety risks, it is in agriculture that the consequences are greatest with the destruction, in 2019, of maize and pea crops to the tune of 1 million euros in Finistère and almost as much in Côtes-d'Armor and Morbihan. Every year, exceptional permits for collection, regulation and firing are revised upwards, yet the gregarious instinct of this species persists and there is a real invasion. Pending the conclusion in 2022 of a study carried out by the Regional Directorate for the Environment, Planning and Housing, on the one hand, and the development of cage trapping experiments on the other hand, farmers need support. They cannot wait two years without their loss of profits being taken into account through temporary compensation. We also propose that you move EUR 3 million towards support for farmers, by reducing the appropriations from Action 6 of the "Safety and sanitary qualities of food" programme in order to abound a new programme "Management of crises and hazards of agricultural production". This amendment was defended in the framework of PLFR 4, but it was rejected on the grounds that it did not fall within the scope of the national fund for risk management in agriculture. It is therefore with a certain solemnity that I invite you to vote for this amendment.

The Secours populaire estimates that 21% of French people do not have the means to eat healthy food, that 27% of them do not have the financial means to buy fruit and vegetables on a daily basis. The cheque I defend the introduction of must give access to healthy, local and ecological food and is therefore intended for producers, farmers in transition, through short circuits or other circuits. It is therefore a worthy cheque that gives access to a healthy diet while supporting the agro-ecological transition of producers. By voting on this amendment, you will allow for a large-scale pilot experiment in 2021, which should lead to a generalisation in 2022. You're going to tell me that the next climate bill will make this vote unnecessary as the amendment will be satisfied – except that we'll be looking at this bill in several months and we'll have wasted all that time. So, by voting on this amendment, you will allow a large-scale experiment to be carried out in 2021, allowing a generalization in 2022, without waiting for the adoption of the climate law, which it will promote. The French Cardiology Federation thus establishes that young French people have lost, in forty years, 25% of their cardiovascular functional abilities. This is why the school has become more than ever a must-have place for every student to have access to a high-quality sports and artistic practice. With this amendment, we propose an increase of EUR 5 million for both first-level education and second-level education. Our students will get started and even, if I dare say so, will be in the race!

We can therefore only encourage this questioning and, therefore, demand that it be followed by acts. Indeed, the derisory duration of the trainings has terrible consequences for everyone, both citizens and police officers. Researcher Sébastien Roché also notes that unlike other countries, such as Denmark or Germany, where training is longer and the emphasis is placed on interaction with the population and the need to use force as little as possible to gain confidence, the training of police officers in France is focused on technical gestures, to the detriment of relational and ethical skills. It is therefore necessary to review both the duration and the content of the formations, so that, when they leave, our Republican police are given better trained peacekeepers.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u841	If I may, Mr President, I will jointly defend Amendments Nos 475, 837 and 474. The health crisis has severely and sustainably affected the tourism sector in general, particularly leisure, attraction and cultural areas, which are characterized by important maintenance obligations during closure periods, as well as heavy annual investments. Their financial situation at the end of 2020 calls into question the sustainability of many of these areas, which are anchored in our territories and play a key role in their vitality. In the event of closure – inevitable if strong measures are not taken quickly – it is the entire local economic fabric that will be reached, with all the consequences that one can fear, namely dismissals, the absence of seasonal workers, a decline in the activity of suppliers and subcontractors, and even cascade closures, which will further penalise already very fragile territories. While the measures taken to date by the public authorities, in particular the Tourism Plan, the general assistance measures and, lastly, the expansion of the solidarity fund, have to some extent mitigated the most immediate negative effects of the crisis on leisure, attraction and cultural spaces, they do not take into account their specificities. These measures are therefore insufficient and largely inadequate, in particular to support medium-sized parks in great difficulty. Indeed, for most of these spaces, the opening season to the public lasts only from 1 April to 30 September, and no sales are made during the interseason. This period is crucial, since it allows permanent staff in these spaces to maintain sometimes complex infrastructure, such as amusement parks, to comply with regulatory safety requirements, to feed animals in animal parks and, in all cases, to undertake renewal work and to prepare for the next season. Amendment No 475 is therefore intended to create an equalisation fund to restore equality between leisure, attraction and cultural areas and other tourist sectors in support of partial activity in the year 2021. The proposed compensation would take the form of State support for part of the gross wage of employees who cannot be placed in partial employment...

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u905	I propose that, through my office, you meet directly with Agnes Pannier-Runacher to discuss with you the latest developments relating to this company, some of which are confidential. With regard to Mr Woerth's amendment, I agree with the rapporteur-general's arguments; Forgive me for not repeating them. As far as neighbourhoods under city policy are concerned, the Prime Minister has pledged at least EUR 1 billion in support. However, it is not a question of creating a programme that allocates the funds of the recovery plan to a particular neighbourhood: we have not done so in any territory. Instead, it is a matter of ensuring that these appropriations are correctly directed towards the territories concerned; the account will be there for all the devices, and we will have the opportunity to verify it. Don't worry, Mr. Little: it's not a question of creating a programme, but of ensuring that these territories, like the others, benefit greatly from the recovery plan and the mechanisms that we are putting in place. I come to indoor and outdoor sports, and to compensation for their fixed charges. We have considerably strengthened the solidarity fund. For example, companies that will remain closed in the forecast period will receive an allowance of EUR 10000, or equivalent to 20% of their turnover and capped at EUR 200000. It turns out that some are not covered by this scheme, either because the ceiling of EUR 200000 is too low – for very large parks, in particular – or because the rate of 20% of turnover is too low and does not cover their fixed charges. Beyond the size effect, situations are extremely heterogeneous. The fixed loads of zoos, for example, are not as high as we imagine, unlike those of circuses – but this issue can be resolved by decree. Small indoor parks, especially for children, rather than large sports parks, have the highest fixed load rates. Since this sector is very heterogeneous, it is difficult to draw up a regulation that covers all situations, without weakening the building of the solidarity fund, whose coherence we want to preserve. We have been facing this difficulty for several weeks. However, work has been carried out: the rapporteur-general cited the initiatives of Members Alexandre Holroyd, Stella Dupont, Daniel Labaronne and Cendra Motin, who still met our teams in Bercy a few hours ago. As the rapporteur-general has pointed out, all the parliamentary groups have alerted us to the difficulties faced by the players in the indoor and outdoor sectors. President Woerth has rightly said that this subject has been the subject of sustained attention since the PLFR2 – I have witnessed it for the indoor, given our neighbourhood on the bench. We made the decision, with Bruno Le Maire, to carry out a work of lace, of "sewn hand". We will benefit both from the emergency-related appropriations – which we propose to vote for – and from the possibility of acting by regulation in order to open a one-stop shop, centralized in Bercy. We will receive each of the actors concerned: their small number allows us to meet them individually, if they are large, or together, to provide them with solutions on a case-by-case basis. This is the commitment made by my teams and by Bruno Le Maire, whom I confirm in front of you. This will be the best way to find solutions that are viable, tailor-made and precisely tailored to each situation, while a regulation would not have covered the diversity of configurations. [[Applause on a few benches of the LaREM group]] My opinion is therefore unfavourable on the whole of the amendments, with the exception, of course, of that of the Government.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u951	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u952	Very favourable opinion. I welcome the Government's initiative to decide in this new reading – in an unusual way in which everyone will understand the reasons – to replenish the appropriations of the mission "Emergency plan in the face of the health crisis", the amount of which was still zero before this amendment. It goes without saying that the carry-overs from 2020 are insufficient in relation to the needs of the first months of 2021. These funds, as proposed by the Government, are primarily intended to extend the Solidarity Fund because it is effective. It allows companies to receive aid of up to EUR 10000 per month or 20% of their monthly turnover if it is less than EUR 200000. It is a lot of public money, of course, but this expenditure is necessary to save all these companies, as illustrated by the cases cited in the previous mission. I welcome the Government's willingness to anticipate this useful and well-targeted funding. Let us avoid any misunderstanding: under no circumstances should these appropriations anticipate further closures other than those we know. Let's be clear: there is no provision on a third wave. The appropriations we are voting on relate to the end of the second confinement and the gradual reopening of all activities – which we hope to see as soon as possible.

More than ever, for the dignity of the people of France and to meet their basic needs, free water, indispensable for life, is required. This measure would also help to combat poverty – at a time when it is exploding – as it would alleviate constraints on household budgets. As water is a scarce resource, it is important that the essential part of life – especially the one we drink – is not charged at the same price as the water used, for example, to fill a swimming pool.

Of course, of course, there is the tax credit voted in the Senate through an amendment that created section 42 unvicies. It is not a tax credit at the moment I speak to you, since it will only be so when you raise the pledge; For the time being, it is only a tax reduction. However, as the article is drafted, there are several flaws in it, including the fact that farmers in polyculture and livestock farming cannot benefit from it – we propose several amendments to remedy them. But above all, it allows those who have been involved for some time in the transition to use this tax credit. This article is intended to accompany those who need it more. The idea is, therefore, to clarify things, for greater fairness, knowing that fairness must be achieved with all operators, including multi-culturators.

The aim is to encourage a producer who is considering a big film project abroad to locate it in France. The international tax credit is a tax incentive rather than a stimulus. ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1270	Let me say a few words on this important subject, Mr President. It is proposed here to re-establish the original wording of Article 44d, which provides for the centralisation of the collection of various taxes of the Directorate-General for Customs and Indirect Duties within the DGFIP. The agents are concerned, rightly, and already there are difficulties. Thus, a change in the base of the francisation right and the operative event would result in a loss of revenue for the community of Corsica, but also for the managers of the ports and for the security companies. The specific reduced rate of Corsica represents nearly 5 million paid annually to the community and contributes to the development of the island. It should also be noted that by cancelling the Corsican rate, Article 44d disregards the rights of the Corsican community, which intervenes in the field of the environment and economic development, in accordance with its powers. Finally, the transfer of the arrangements for recovery creates uncertainty as to the future of the jobs of the customs services in Corsica, which have fallen sharply over the last fifteen years. For all these reasons, I ask the House to oppose the reinstatement of the text in its original version.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1271	I have before me three identical amendments, Nos 181, 403 and 893, which tend to delete Article 44d. Amendment No 181 by Mrs Lise Magnier is being defended. Mr Loïc Prud'homme has the floor to support Amendment No. 403.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1272	It is intended to cancel the transfer to FIPB of the management of the recovery and control of the CTBTP. This exercise is an exemplary illustration of the Government's harmful methods, thereby shifting provisions that are contrary to the public interest. While Article 13 of the Finance Bill for 2021 deals with the transfer of taxes from the Directorate-General of Customs and Indirect Duties to the DGFIP, the Government is introducing a catimini amendment to cut any debate. The ICTPE generates €33 billion in revenue each year. Its management and control are the specific know-how of customs. The Government succeeded in reaching unanimity against him. From the SNCD – National Union of Customs Officers – to the CGT through the CFDT, all customs unions stand up against this decision, not by corporatist reflex, but in a healthy movement to defend the general interest. Customs is the most suitable service for the treatment of TICEP. It is able to guarantee tax revenues and, above all, to ensure the traceability and control of the products concerned, which DGFIP cannot and cannot do. This transfer is therefore dangerous for public finances and for us, the citizens, who will bear the costs of the potential frauds that this transfer will entail. We are also concerned about the risk of the customs break that this measure poses: the transfer of powers can be used as a subsequent pretext by the Government to undermine the human and financial resources of this very useful administration, particularly in the light of the CTBTE.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1340	On 9 September, the Constitutional Council declared contrary to the Constitution the provisions which made it possible for the Commission for the Litigation of Paid Parking – CCSP – to make an appeal against a post-parking package subject to prior payment of the fee. The concern for the proper administration of justice required that the provisions concerned be re-established as soon as possible in a form consistent with the requirements of the Constitutional Council's decision. This is what we have done at first reading, by introducing Article 54 quater, which limits prior payment and introduces exceptions. On the basis of earlier censorship of a similar provision, the Senate held that it was a horseman and decided to delete the article. Through this amendment, I propose to re-establish this article in a slightly improved wording of the text resulting from our work at first reading. However, I can see clearly the legal and technical difficulties of the arrangements that I propose. However, I remain convinced that there is a need for a legislative response to the Constitutional Council's decision. I appeal to the Minister-in-Office to know his position on the proposal that I make in this amendment, which the Committee on Finance has adopted.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1350	It aims to clarify what is known as the economic viability of the legal entity producing electricity, taken into account in this section of the draft finance law. The current wording of the article is limited to analysing the economic viability of the producer, referring only to the producer's expenses, without taking into account the borrowing costs borne by the partners in the production structure. The objective is to have a more comprehensive analysis of the photovoltaic project concerned by the article.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1363	For him, I will ask for the attention of my colleagues because it does not cost much while being interesting for the sector: it is a good message that we can send him. Article 54 sexies provides for a safeguard clause on a case-by-case basis. This clause, which the photovoltaic power producer can operate, covers situations where the reduction of the rate proposed by the Department would compromise the economic viability of the existing facility. The amendment makes this safeguard clause effective by making it suspensive for a period of six months or until the publication of a derogatory tariff order. In fact, it is a question of taking a six-month break, the time to look on a case-by-case basis at the viability of the project in the sector concerned. This downtime will allow the situation of each company to be analysed.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1364	Subamendments No 1287 by Mrs Lise Magnier and No 1293 by Mrs Véronique Louwagie are being defended. What is the opinion of the committee on all these amendments and sub-amendments?
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1365	Why should we go back to the version of this article that we adopted at first reading? Because the changes we made to him had allowed for progress and adjustments. The argument of the credibility of the state is understood, but, in my view, the key factor in the decision to be taken remains the evolution of investment costs. In 2006, Italy also revised tariffs, setting the threshold at 200 kilowatts. France is therefore not alone in taking this decision and the evolution of investment costs is not unique to our country. So it's a good decision. At this stage of the discussion of the text, the amendments focus on agricultural installations. What type of installation is targeted? Photovoltaic plants with a capacity of 250 kilowatts occupy an area of 2500 square meters, which represents about two thirds of a football field. With regard to the non-interconnected areas – ZNI – some 200 installations would be concerned. We must therefore reassure everyone: we are targeting the important installations, and we have achieved this result thanks in particular to the adoption of Mrs Benin's amendment. It should also be recalled that the derogation procedure is extremely important throughout the process and allows each specific case to be examined. Some ideas might be interesting, such as Mr Cellier's idea of replacing the concept of capital invested with that of capital fixed. I see a very clear disadvantage: by including fixed capital, one includes, you will agree, the producer's liability, therefore his net borrowing expenses, which creates a difficulty in relation to the object of the article. Therefore, this idea does not seem desirable. Mr Besson-Moreau asked whether, for the borrowing charge, the situation of the producer was taken into account only or whether the holding company or a structure above him was being targeted. In my opinion – I'm talking under control about what the minister delegate will say, which may give some clarification or contradict me – we have to stop at the producer, otherwise the assembly would be too complex. I am also concerned that the possibility of suspending the reduction in the purchase price during the processing of the request for an exemption is another good idea. If everyone did, the operation would become, so to speak, ordinary law and would no longer produce the expected effects. I do not trust this proposal and therefore give an unfavorable opinion. I will conclude by giving you a very concrete example showing that it is appropriate to relativize the idea, agitated like a scarecrow, that no one would want to invest in photovoltaics because of this measure. Last week, the Norwegian manufacturer REC Group announced its intention to set up a giant solar panel plant in Hambach, Moselle, with 1,500 key jobs starting in 2022, even though the provisions contained in this article were already known. Again, France is not the first country, and probably not the last, to make contractual revisions. These, of course, raise legal questions and it is well known that they will give rise to litigation. But I think it is necessary to go back to the version of the text adopted at first reading because it already includes secure provisions. There is a need to reassure farmers about the target, the operators likely to be affected, and this applies equally to non-interconnected areas. The opinion is therefore unfavourable on all the amendments, except that of the committee, which aims to restore the article in its version adopted at first reading, as well as on the sub-amendments.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1366	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1367	In order to do so quickly, I agree with the rapporteur-general in every respect, including the fact that only the producer's situation must be taken into account, because going beyond that would expose us to difficulties. With regard to Mr Cellier's proposal to guarantee to the producers concerned the possibility of benefiting from the individual analysis and therefore from the safeguard clauses before the entry into force of the new tariff, I undertake, and the Government through me, to allow a fairly long period between the publication of the tariff texts and the application of the first reduction. So the work on defining the new tariffs can continue – which is in response to the rapporteur-general's fear, which I share – but this time will allow us to examine the safeguard clause that a producer can claim if he fears being put in trouble by the new tariff. That is our commitment to the method. The opinion is therefore unfavourable on the whole series of amendments, with the exception of that of the committee, as well as on the sub-amendments.

Finally, Amendment No 410, which incorporates another proposal from the CCFD-Terre solidaire association, aims to condition aid for the publication of a country-by-country synthesis report of the activities of the subsidiaries of the companies. The association has shown that subsidiaries of companies which have benefited from measures to take care of partial unemployment, such as Atos, Michelin or Capgemini, have 20,5 %, 17 % and 18 % respectively of their subsidiaries in tax havens. The list of these companies is not exhaustive at all and it is very expensive: in direct effect alone, corporate tax abuses amount to $14 billion each year.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1412	The next speaker is the Minister Delegate, to give the Government's opinion on the amendment and to support Subamendment No. 1269.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1413	The pact signed between the State and the regions provides for the reform of the equalization mechanism in 2022. The Government's sub-amendment merely seeks to delete the reference to the amount of revenue allocated to the fund, so that the level of the fund is defined at the time of the consultation, which will take place in the year 2021, according to the mechanism adopted. It is therefore a sub-amendment competing with that of President Woerth.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1414	What is the Committee's opinion on the sub-amendments?
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1415	Disfavorable to the amendment of the chairman of the commission, to which we prefer that of the Government.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1416	That's too bad!
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1417	Mr Jean-René Cazeneuve has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1418	I will abstain from the Government's sub-amendment. I can understand his position because the minimum amount of equalization for the regions in 2022 is not included in the agreement with the State. However, frankly, asking the regions to commit themselves to equalization without giving them any indication of the minimum rate or the date on which they will have to abound in the fund seems to me a little big. In fact, until now, the regions have not been able to set up a solidarity fund for themselves so that the richest regions can help the poorest. [[Applause on several benches of the LaREM group]] I therefore call on national representation next year: we parliamentarians will ensure that the level of equalisation is high enough to allow genuine solidarity between regions, and that they do not escape this important rule of our social cohesion.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1419	I agree!

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1427	Mrs. Christine Pires Beaune has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1428	Although it is late and speaking of logarithmic coefficient at five and a half in the morning is not very nice, I would like to point out that, following the Government's report to Parliament, I had tabled an amendment on this subject which concluded that certain provisions needed to be re-evaluated. However, between the readings of the National Assembly and the Senate, nothing happened. I therefore understand my honourable senator who passed an amendment to reduce the logarithmic coefficient from 1 to 1.6 to 1 to 2 in the version originally adopted by the House: while I was proposing to proceed gradually, she plans to do so at one time. I would like to draw the attention of all my colleagues to a problem that has not occurred since yesterday. For years, the Association des maires rurales de France has criticized the logarithmic coefficient, which is used not only to calculate the allocation of the lump sum share in the DGF, but also to calculate the FPIC – the national fund for equalisation of intercommunal and communal resources – to achieve differences in the basic allocation that can go from simple to double. The Government's report makes this clear: the logarithmic coefficient puts municipalities with fewer than 500 inhabitants and municipalities with more than 200,000 inhabitants at a disadvantage. Moreover, when we introduced the logarithmic coefficient in 2005, the intercommunal map was not the one we know today: centrality charges are no longer borne by the same communities as at the time. It is therefore urgent to review the logarithmic coefficient. That is why I will support the article introduced by the Senate, even if it is imperfect and we do not have a full impact study on this subject. I shall therefore vote against Amendment No 853.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1429	I'll do the same!
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1430	Mr Christophe Jerretie has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1431	It is indeed a recurring and complex subject which we will have to deal with in the near future. We really need to move forward because the logarithm has changed a lot: the differential effects that were quite legitimate when centrality was ensured by the central town are no longer so since the centrality loads are due to the EPCIs. Logarithm must therefore take its full place in the new funding mechanism. To prepare for the future, we must work to review the logarithmic effect, which is no longer to the taste of the day and is no longer suitable for our territories. Having said that, I will vote in favour of the committee's amendment. However, from what I have seen, the consequences of the logarithmic coefficient vary widely across communities, and so we need to work on the subject to assess these consequences more accurately. It seems to me very urgent to move forward on this matter.

ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1441	The rapporteur-general has the floor to support Amendment No. 602.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1442	Since the entry into force of the NOTRE law – the new territorial organization of the Republic – we have had this discussion during each review of a draft finance law: it is about the distribution of taxation and revenue between the Greater Paris metropolis and territorial public institutions. In short, we decided, at first reading, in view of the current distribution of powers, to favour the preservation by the territorial public institutions of the tax tools which ensure the revenue necessary for their operation, while ensuring that the metropolitan area is not too affected by the foreseeable decline of the CVAE, its main tax instrument for the year 2021. We had therefore proposed that the dynamics of the CFE – business property assessment – go back from the EFA to the metropolis. Since the first reading, the estimate of the loss of revenue from the CVAE that the Greater Paris metropolis will suffer has been revised downwards. In addition, a pertinent remark from Gilles Carrez convinced us of the need to involve the city of Paris in this scheme, which was not mentioned in the first draft of the finance bill. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM group. – Exclaims on various benches]] The aim of this amendment is therefore to ensure that the city of Paris contributes with the territorial public institutions to the return to the metropolis of the dynamics of the CFE; to reduce this recovery to two thirds of what we had set at first reading, taking into account the new estimate of the losses of the metropolis. These new provisions will be much more favourable to territorial public institutions, the territories of Greater Paris and the elected representatives of those territories; That is what it is important to remember, and I have no doubt that those of our colleagues who are not from the little crown, or even from the francilians, will nevertheless approve of my remarks.
ParlaMint-FR_2020-12-15-O1122.u1443	Indeed, the minister delegate, who is not a francilian, clearly agrees!
